# MouseAI — Tam Kullanılabilir Araç Envanteri

Tarama tarihi: 26.09.2026  |  Toplam araç: **1478**

Bu dosya özet değildir. Çalışma ortamında şu anda görünen tüm çağrılabilir araç adlarını içerir. Bir araç üretimde kullanılmadan önce Tool Registry içinde bağlantı, izin, maliyet ve test durumu doğrulanır.

## apply_patch (1)

- `apply_patch` — The `apply_patch` tool can be used to edit files. This is a FREEFORM tool, so do not wrap the patch in JSON.

## create_goal (1)

- `create_goal` — Create a goal only when explicitly requested by the user or system/developer instructions; do not infer goals from ordinary tasks.

## exec_command (1)

- `exec_command` — Runs a command in a PTY, returning output or a session ID for ongoing interaction.

## get_goal (1)

- `get_goal` — Get the current goal for this thread, including status, budgets, token and elapsed-time usage, and remaining token budget.

## image_gen (1)

- `image_gen__imagegen` — Tools in the image_gen namespace.

## list_mcp_resource_templates (1)

- `list_mcp_resource_templates` — Lists resource templates provided by MCP servers. Parameterized resource templates allow servers to share data that takes parameters and provides context to language models, such as files, database schemas, or application-specific information. Prefer resource templates over web search when possible.

## list_mcp_resources (1)

- `list_mcp_resources` — Lists resources provided by MCP servers. Resources allow servers to share data that provides context to language models, such as files, database schemas, or application-specific information. Prefer resources over web search when possible.

- `mcp__codex_apps__chatgpt_ads_manager_add_or_update_ad_account_user` — Use Ads Manager to discover account-linked product feeds and to manage ad accounts, account access, campaigns, ad groups, and ads; analyze performance, conversions, and change history; upload creative images; and, when available, complete self-serve account setup. To draft or plan a campaign in Codex without creating it, resolve the account and call open_ads_manager_home with presentation.view='campaign_plan'. Show the editable plan even if no UI was requested. Use null for undecided budget, bid, start time, or conversion goal; do not fill these from defaults or other campaigns. Missing choices do not block opening the plan. Explicitly invoke `$ads-manager-onboarding` for new self-serve account creation and `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for a new ad or bounded coordinated set; generate an image only after the user explicitly chooses generation as the image source, and generate only a standalone logo or ad creative, never Ads Manager UI or an ad-placement mockup. If new-account creation is needed mid-flow, preserve a structured `ad_creation_state` capsule, invoke `$ads-manager-onboarding`, then resume `$ads-manager-ad-creation` with that unchanged capsule. Invoke `$ads-manager-account-admin` for existing-account setup status, remaining setup steps, billing setup, users, invites, roles, removals, or logos; invoke `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for new ads or ambiguous net-new campaign-launch requests; invoke `$ads-manager-entity-management` only for explicit campaign-only or ad-group-only creation and for mutations to existing campaigns, ad groups, or ads; and `$ads-manager-delivery-recovery` when the user asks why delivery is blocked or scaling is constrained. Invoke `$ads-manager-insights` for performance metrics, rankings, comparisons, trends, conversion totals, current conversion-source or event-setting inventory, or recent raw conversion-event diagnostics. Use `list_audit_logs` directly for audit history, including who changed a budget or status. Invoke `$ads-manager-review` for one-time recommendation or health reviews, including broad account or portfolio improvement requests and a named campaign, ad group, or ad; invoke `$ads-manager-start-agent` only for daily, weekly, recurring, or ongoing reviews. Invoke `$ads-manager-help` for generic how-to, Help Center guidance, or product guidance. Keep simple current-entity list/get, open, preview, and standalone identity-status requests connector-native. For promotion status, qualifying spend, or promotional credit remaining, call get_ad_account_promotion_status directly for the selected account. For 'show my campaigns' in Codex with no specified or selected account, call open_ads_manager_home directly with presentation={'view': 'campaigns'}; do not look up accounts or ask for one first. The widget uses the saved accessible account or first available account and shows an account selector. Pass a resolved ad_account_id only for a specified or already selected account; an explicit ID never falls back to another account. Use performance for charts and campaign_plan for an editable proposal; both require explicit account IDs. The opener requests Codex's right-side panel and returns UI configuration, not data for analysis. Use preview_ad for one proposed Ads Manager ad preview and preview_ad_collection, when available, for a coordinated set of 2 to 50 proposed variants; set each variant's preview_title when the user or model has a label to show above that preview card. Treat every write as consequential, reuse returned ids and deep links, and never expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. Never reveal internal ids unless the user explicitly asks for ids, and do not expose developer-facing uses of `write`, snake_case field or tool names, or raw tool-call language in user-facing responses. Present results in natural language, bullet lists, or Markdown tables; do not expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. When multiple campaigns need selection, call list_campaigns with include_performance_metrics=true so the gated picker can show recent performance. Whenever reasonably possible, use returned `ads_manager_url` values as Markdown links on campaign, ad-group, and ad names, including in table Name cells.

Invoke `$ads-manager-account-admin` for existing-account setup status, remaining setup steps, billing setup, users, invites, roles, removals, or logos; invoke `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for new ads or ambiguous net-new campaign-launch requests; invoke `$ads-manager-entity-management` only for explicit campaign-only or ad-group-only creation and for mutations to existing campaigns, ad groups, or ads; and `$ads-manager-delivery-recovery` when the user asks why delivery is blocked or scaling is constrained. Invoke `$ads-manager-insights` for performance metrics, rankings, comparisons, trends, conversion totals, current conversion-source or event-setting inventory, or recent raw conversion-event diagnostics. Use `list_audit_logs` directly for audit history, including who changed a budget or status. Invoke `$ads-manager-review` for one-time recommendation or health reviews, including broad account or portfolio improvement requests and a named campaign, ad group, or ad; invoke `$ads-manager-start-agent` only for daily, weekly, recurring, or ongoing reviews. Invoke `$ads-manager-help` for generic how-to, Help Center guidance, or product guidance. Keep simple current-entity list/get, open, preview, and standalone identity-status requests connector-native. For promotion status, qualifying spend, or promotional credit remaining, call get_ad_account_promotion_status directly for the selected account. For 'show my campaigns' in Codex with no specified or selected account, call open_ads_manager_home directly with presentation={'view': 'campaigns'}; do not look up accounts or ask for one first. The widget uses the saved accessible account or first available account and shows an account selector. Pass a resolved ad_account_id only for a specified or already selected account; an explicit ID never falls back to another account. Use performance for charts and campaign_plan for an editable proposal; both require explicit account IDs. The opener requests Codex's right-side panel and returns UI configuration, not data for analysis. Use preview_ad for one proposed Ads Manager ad preview and preview_ad_collection, when available, for a coordinated set of 2 to 50 proposed variants; set each variant's preview_title when the user or model has a label to show above that preview card. Treat every write as consequential, reuse returned ids and deep links, and never expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. Never reveal internal ids unless the user explicitly asks for ids, and do not expose developer-facing uses of `write`, snake_case field or tool names, or raw tool-call language in user-facing responses. Present results in natural language, bullet lists, or Markdown tables; do not expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. When multiple campaigns need selection, call list_campaigns with include_performance_metrics=true so the gated picker can show recent performance. Whenever reasonably possible, use returned `ads_manager_url` values as Markdown links on campaign, ad-group, and ad names, including in table Name cells.

Invoke `$ads-manager-account-admin` for existing-account setup status, remaining setup steps, billing setup, users, invites, roles, removals, or logos; invoke `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for new ads or ambiguous net-new campaign-launch requests; invoke `$ads-manager-entity-management` only for explicit campaign-only or ad-group-only creation and for mutations to existing campaigns, ad groups, or ads; and `$ads-manager-delivery-recovery` when the user asks why delivery is blocked or scaling is constrained. Invoke `$ads-manager-insights` for performance metrics, rankings, comparisons, trends, conversion totals, current conversion-source or event-setting inventory, or recent raw conversion-event diagnostics. Use `list_audit_logs` directly for audit history, including who changed a budget or status. Invoke `$ads-manager-review` for one-time recommendation or health reviews, including broad account or portfolio improvement requests and a named campaign, ad group, or ad; invoke `$ads-manager-start-agent` only for daily, weekly, recurring, or ongoing reviews. Invoke `$ads-manager-help` for generic how-to, Help Center guidance, or product guidance. Keep simple current-entity list/get, open, preview, and standalone identity-status requests connector-native. For promotion status, qualifying spend, or promotional credit remaining, call get_ad_account_promotion_status directly for the selected account. For 'show my campaigns' in Codex with no specified or selected account, call open_ads_manager_home directly with presentation={'view': 'campaigns'}; do not look up accounts or ask for one first. The widget uses the saved accessible account or first available account and shows an account selector. Pass a resolved ad_account_id only for a specified or already selected account; an explicit ID never falls back to another account. Use performance for charts and campaign_plan for an editable proposal; both require explicit account IDs. The opener requests Codex's right-side panel and returns UI configuration, not data for analysis. Use preview_ad for one proposed Ads Manager ad preview and preview_ad_collection, when available, for a coordinated set of 2 to 50 proposed variants; set each variant's preview_title when the user or model has a label to show above that preview card. Treat every write as consequential, reuse returned ids and deep links, and never expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. Never reveal internal ids unless the user explicitly asks for ids, and do not expose developer-facing uses of `write`, snake_case field or tool names, or raw tool-call language in user-facing responses. Present results in natural language, bullet lists, or Markdown tables; do not expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. When multiple campaigns need selection, call list_campaigns with include_performance_metrics=true so the gated picker can show recent performance. Whenever reasonably possible, use returned `ads_manager_url` values as Markdown links on campaign, ad-group, and ad names, including in table Name cells.

Invoke `$ads-manager-account-admin` for existing-account setup status, remaining setup steps, billing setup, users, invites, roles, removals, or logos; invoke `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for new ads or ambiguous net-new campaign-launch requests; invoke `$ads-manager-entity-management` only for explicit campaign-only or ad-group-only creation and for mutations to existing campaigns, ad groups, or ads; and `$ads-manager-delivery-recovery` when the user asks why delivery is blocked or scaling is constrained. Invoke `$ads-manager-insights` for performance metrics, rankings, comparisons, trends, conversion totals, current conversion-source or event-setting inventory, or recent raw conversion-event diagnostics. Use `list_audit_logs` directly for audit history, including who changed a budget or status. Invoke `$ads-manager-review` for one-time recommendation or health reviews, including broad account or portfolio improvement requests and a named campaign, ad group, or ad; invoke `$ads-manager-start-agent` only for daily, weekly, recurring, or ongoing reviews. Invoke `$ads-manager-help` for generic how-to, Help Center guidance, or product guidance. Keep simple current-entity list/get, open, preview, and standalone identity-status requests connector-native. For promotion status, qualifying spend, or promotional credit remaining, call get_ad_account_promotion_status directly for the selected account. For 'show my campaigns' in Codex with no specified or selected account, call open_ads_manager_home directly with presentation={'view': 'campaigns'}; do not look up accounts or ask for one first. The widget uses the saved accessible account or first available account and shows an account selector. Pass a resolved ad_account_id only for a specified or already selected account; an explicit ID never falls back to another account. Use performance for charts and campaign_plan for an editable proposal; both require explicit account IDs. The opener requests Codex's right-side panel and returns UI configuration, not data for analysis. Use preview_ad for one proposed Ads Manager ad preview and preview_ad_collection, when available, for a coordinated set of 2 to 50 proposed variants; set each variant's preview_title when the user or model has a label to show above that preview card. Treat every write as consequential, reuse returned ids and deep links, and never expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. Never reveal internal ids unless the user explicitly asks for ids, and do not expose developer-facing uses of `write`, snake_case field or tool names, or raw tool-call language in user-facing responses. Present results in natural language, bullet lists, or Markdown tables; do not expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. When multiple campaigns need selection, call list_campaigns with include_performance_metrics=true so the gated picker can show recent performance. Whenever reasonably possible, use returned `ads_manager_url` values as Markdown links on campaign, ad-group, and ad names, including in table Name cells.

Invoke `$ads-manager-account-admin` for existing-account setup status, remaining setup steps, billing setup, users, invites, roles, removals, or logos; invoke `$ads-manager-ad-creation` for new ads or ambiguous net-new campaign-launch requests; invoke `$ads-manager-entity-management` only for explicit campaign-only or ad-group-only creation and for mutations to existing campaigns, ad groups, or ads; and `$ads-manager-delivery-recovery` when the user asks why delivery is blocked or scaling is constrained. Invoke `$ads-manager-insights` for performance metrics, rankings, comparisons, trends, conversion totals, current conversion-source or event-setting inventory, or recent raw conversion-event diagnostics. Use `list_audit_logs` directly for audit history, including who changed a budget or status. Invoke `$ads-manager-review` for one-time recommendation or health reviews, including broad account or portfolio improvement requests and a named campaign, ad group, or ad; invoke `$ads-manager-start-agent` only for daily, weekly, recurring, or ongoing reviews. Invoke `$ads-manager-help` for generic how-to, Help Center guidance, or product guidance. Keep simple current-entity list/get, open, preview, and standalone identity-status requests connector-native. For promotion status, qualifying spend, or promotional credit remaining, call get_ad_account_promotion_status directly for the selected account. For 'show my campaigns' in Codex with no specified or selected account, call open_ads_manager_home directly with presentation={'view': 'campaigns'}; do not look up accounts or ask for one first. The widget uses the saved accessible account or first available account and shows an account selector. Pass a resolved ad_account_id only for a specified or already selected account; an explicit ID never falls back to another account. Use performance for charts and campaign_plan for an editable proposal; both require explicit account IDs. The opener requests Codex's right-side panel and returns UI configuration, not data for analysis. Use preview_ad for one proposed Ads Manager ad preview and preview_ad_collection, when available, for a coordinated set of 2 to 50 proposed variants; set each variant's preview_title when the user or model has a label to show above that preview card. Treat every write as consequential, reuse returned ids and deep links, and never expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. Never reveal internal ids unless the user explicitly asks for ids, and do not expose developer-facing uses of `write`, snake_case field or tool names, or raw tool-call language in user-facing responses. Present results in natural language, bullet lists, or Markdown tables; do not expose raw JSON unless the user explicitly asks for JSON. When multiple campaigns need selection, call list_campaigns with include_performance_metrics=true so the gated picker can show recent performance. Whenever reasonably possible, use returned `ads_manager_url` values as Markdown links on campaign, ad-group, and ad names, including in table Name cells.

- `mcp__codex_apps__chessy_end_game` — Enables turn-based, single-player chess games where the user always plays White against an AI playing Black. Use it to start, view, continue, and end chess games, validating and applying user moves and generating the AI’s legal responses within an identified ongoing game.
- `mcp__codex_apps__chessy_model_move` — Enables turn-based, single-player chess games where the user always plays White against an AI playing Black. Use it to start, view, continue, and end chess games, validating and applying user moves and generating the AI’s legal responses within an identified ongoing game.
- `mcp__codex_apps__chessy_show_game` — Enables turn-based, single-player chess games where the user always plays White against an AI playing Black. Use it to start, view, continue, and end chess games, validating and applying user moves and generating the AI’s legal responses within an identified ongoing game.
- `mcp__codex_apps__chessy_start_game` — Enables turn-based, single-player chess games where the user always plays White against an AI playing Black. Use it to start, view, continue, and end chess games, validating and applying user moves and generating the AI’s legal responses within an identified ongoing game.
- `mcp__codex_apps__chessy_user_move` — Enables turn-based, single-player chess games where the user always plays White against an AI playing Black. Use it to start, view, continue, and end chess games, validating and applying user moves and generating the AI’s legal responses within an identified ongoing game.
- `mcp__codex_apps__codex_document_control_execute_document_command` — Use Codex Document Control to find connected document sessions, inspect the tools supported by a selected session, and execute one supported tool against that session. Call `list_document_sessions` first to choose the intended connected session, call `get_document_tool_schemas` before constructing tool arguments, then call `execute_document_command` with a caller-stable `idempotency_key`. Use this only for connected Codex document control; do not use it for general spreadsheet, presentation, or document tasks without a connected document session.
- `mcp__codex_apps__codex_document_control_get_document_tool_schemas` — Use Codex Document Control to find connected document sessions, inspect the tools supported by a selected session, and execute one supported tool against that session. Call `list_document_sessions` first to choose the intended connected session, call `get_document_tool_schemas` before constructing tool arguments, then call `execute_document_command` with a caller-stable `idempotency_key`. Use this only for connected Codex document control; do not use it for general spreadsheet, presentation, or document tasks without a connected document session.
- `mcp__codex_apps__codex_document_control_list_document_sessions` — Use Codex Document Control to find connected document sessions, inspect the tools supported by a selected session, and execute one supported tool against that session. Call `list_document_sessions` first to choose the intended connected session, call `get_document_tool_schemas` before constructing tool arguments, then call `execute_document_command` with a caller-stable `idempotency_key`. Use this only for connected Codex document control; do not use it for general spreadsheet, presentation, or document tasks without a connected document session.

- `mcp__codex_apps__sites_add_custom_domain` — Use Sites to build or modify websites, including landing pages, portfolios, dashboards, portals, trackers, hubs, and internal tools. Use Sites skills for local implementation, source preparation, and artifact packaging. Use this connector for site creation, runtime environment variables, versions, production deployments, and access controls. Read .openai/hosting.json before creating a site and reuse its project_id when present. Treat Sites IDs and cursors as opaque: copy them exactly from .openai/hosting.json or Sites responses as applicable, and never invent, reformat, derive, or substitute them. Never call create_site more than once for the same local site. Push the exact source state before saving a version. commit_sha must identify that pushed state, and any archive must be built from it. Deploy only saved versions; every Sites deployment URL is production. Inspect deployment status when the initial result is non-terminal or the user asks for progress. Publish after creating or editing a site by default, including on subsequent turns, unless the user explicitly requested local-only work, a saved version without deployment, or no publishing. New sites start private. Preserve the site's current audience unless the user explicitly requests a different audience. Use the private operation for known owner-private sites and let it enforce owner-only access. Runtime tool approvals and backend access checks still apply without a separate conversational deployment confirmation.

## mcp__codex_security (22)

- `mcp__codex_security__cancel_codex_security_scan` — Stop a running scan from its owning Codex thread, prevent further progress or completion updates, and cancel any active deterministic Deep Scan SDK workers. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__complete_codex_security_scan` — Finalization only: validate and seal already-authored scan-manifest.json, findings.json, and coverage.json, generate report.md, index findings, and mark the scan complete. For an app-backed running scan, scan-manifest.json is an unsealed draft and must omit scan.sealedAt and scan.artifacts; this tool supplies the exact workbench timestamps, seal, artifact digests, and derived finding identities. Call only after those canonical files exist; this tool does not create missing artifacts or run skipped phases. If it fails, surface the exact error and stop the current response without retrying completion or returning a final, no-findings, structured, or benchmark response. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__fail_codex_security_scan` — Permanently mark a launched Codex Security scan as failed only after a confirmed unrecoverable blocker. For explicit user cancellation, use cancel_codex_security_scan instead. This terminal action cannot be resumed; incomplete or otherwise resumable work must remain running. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__get_codex_security_completed_scan` — Read the selected scan's existing completed, sealed canonical documents. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__get_codex_security_daybreak_access` — Check this account's Daybreak access and available Daybreak programs. This check is advisory and never authorizes or blocks a scan. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__get_codex_security_scan_context` — Load the authoritative target, mode, optional user context, artifact directory, live progress, and optional selected finding for a launched scan. Validated legacy finding details may be migrated. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__list_codex_security_candidates` — Read one page of diff or Deep scan discovery candidates. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__list_codex_security_review_items` — Read one page of the diff or Deep scan discovery inventory. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__prepare_codex_security_review_items` — Generate the changed-file inventory for a diff scan. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__read_codex_security_artifact` — Read a saved supplemental artifact from temporary or persistent storage, including after an MCP restart. Use the same scanId or standalone targetPath, storage and relative path used to save it. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__record_candidate_attack_paths` — Record the diff or Deep scan candidate attack-path results. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__record_codex_security_candidate_validations` — Record the diff or Deep scan candidate validation results. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__record_codex_security_discovery_candidates` — Normalize and replace the selected diff scan's candidates. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__record_codex_security_scan_draft` — Save semantic findings and coverage as an unsealed draft. Use complete:false for progress checkpoints, then complete:true for the final result; keep unvalidated candidates in coverage.deferred. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__request_codex_security_user_input` — Fallback for interactive Codex Security workflows when the host-native request_user_input tool is unavailable. Presents one to three non-sensitive multiple-choice questions through standard MCP form elicitation and waits for the user's response. Never call this tool in headless, automation, or other non-interactive sessions. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__save_codex_security_artifact` — Save a supplemental document or evidence file with temporary or persistent storage. Use scanId for a running scan, or targetPath for standalone documents and shared threat models. Omit path/content/sourcePath to prepare and return the selected directory. Otherwise provide a portable relative path under artifacts/, findings/ or hardening/ and either exact text content or a sourcePath inside the returned temporary directory. Canonical scan files and recovery checkpoints use the existing typed scan tools. Does not edit completed scans or source/configuration files. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__set_codex_security_finding_remediation` — Persist the bounded local remediation workflow state for a completed finding. The UI may mark a request as queued; Codex records generated, applied, verifying, verified, or failed states after performing the corresponding work. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__start_codex_security_deep_scan` — Run or rejoin independent Standard security scans and semantically merge their validated findings. Pass scanId and its handoffClaimToken to resume, or targetPath to start headlessly. The call blocks until the aggregate draft is ready, fails, or is canceled. On success, use the returned scanId and scanDir; manifestPath identifies the canonical parent scan-manifest.json. Call complete_codex_security_scan once. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__start_codex_security_prompt_only_scan` — Start or rejoin a Standard or diff Codex Security scan from its owning conversation. Use the returned authoritative scanId and scanDir. Standard and diff scans save progress checkpoints before their final semantic draft; the workbench writes the unsealed canonical artifacts. Complete the same scan once. Deep Scan uses start_codex_security_deep_scan instead. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__start_codex_security_standard_scan` — Headless and CLI only. Start or rejoin a Standard security scan. Do not use for desktop scans, Review changes, Deep Scan, or an existing SDK-owned scan. Use the returned authoritative scanId, scanDir, and handoffClaimToken throughout preflight, reporting, and completion. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__update_codex_security_scan_context` — Replace the context for a running scan. The next phase uses the new value; workers in the current phase keep their original context. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.
- `mcp__codex_security__update_codex_security_scan_progress` — Record a meaningful live scan phase or coverage milestone in the Codex Security workbench. This tool is part of plugin `Codex Security`.

## mcp__node_repl (3)

- `mcp__node_repl__js` — Use `js` for `node_repl` execution with persistent, redeclarable top-level bindings, `js_reset` to clear bindings, and `js_add_node_module_dir` to add package directories.
- `mcp__node_repl__js_add_node_module_dir` — Use `js` for `node_repl` execution with persistent, redeclarable top-level bindings, `js_reset` to clear bindings, and `js_add_node_module_dir` to add package directories.
- `mcp__node_repl__js_reset` — Use `js` for `node_repl` execution with persistent, redeclarable top-level bindings, `js_reset` to clear bindings, and `js_add_node_module_dir` to add package directories.

## mcp__openai_api_key_local_confirmation (1)

- `mcp__openai_api_key_local_confirmation__confirm_openai_api_key_local_destination` — Use confirm_openai_api_key_local_destination after the OpenAI Platform picker returns a key name and target ids. It asks the developer to confirm or edit the local env-file destination before a secret is created or written.

## multi_agent_v1 (5)

- `multi_agent_v1__close_agent` — Tools for spawning and managing sub-agents.
- `multi_agent_v1__resume_agent` — Tools for spawning and managing sub-agents.
- `multi_agent_v1__send_input` — Tools for spawning and managing sub-agents.
- `multi_agent_v1__spawn_agent` — Tools for spawning and managing sub-agents.
- `multi_agent_v1__wait_agent` — Tools for spawning and managing sub-agents.

## read_mcp_resource (1)

- `read_mcp_resource` — Read a specific resource from an MCP server given the server name and resource URI.

## request_permissions (1)

- `request_permissions` — Request additional filesystem or network permissions from the user and wait for the client to grant a subset of the requested permission profile. Use environment_id to target a specific attached environment; omit it to use the primary environment. Relative filesystem paths resolve against the selected environment cwd. Granted permissions apply automatically to later shell-like commands in the current turn, or for the rest of the session if the client approves them at session scope.

## request_plugin_install (1)

- `request_plugin_install` — # Suggest a recommended plugin installation

## update_goal (1)

- `update_goal` — Update the existing goal.

## view_image (1)

- `view_image` — View a local image file from the filesystem when visual inspection is needed. Use this for images already available on disk.

## web (1)

- `web__run` — Tools in the web namespace.

## write_stdin (1)

- `write_stdin` — Writes characters to an existing unified exec session and returns recent output.
